- **Flags:** polish; size S
- **Screens:** `p.settings.default.1280`, `p.access-log.default.1280`, `p.correspondence.read-only.1280`
- **Routes:** `/p/[practiceId]/settings`, `/p/[practiceId]/settings/*`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/components/settings/nav.tsx`
- **Evidence:** Each administrator-only tab carries a 10 px 'admin' badge whose meaning, 'Changes here are for the practice administrator', is only its title attribute (components/settings/nav.tsx:44-48): a keyboard or touch user never sees it, and the tab's accessible name becomes 'Proof level admin'.
- **Rule:** WCAG 2.4.6 Headings and Labels (AA); hover-only information is not available by keyboard (2.1.1)
- **Best fix:** Visually hidden text inside the badge ('admin<span class="sr-only">: changed by the practice administrator</span>') and one visible line under the tabs: 'admin: changed by the practice administrator'.

### `a11y-practice-2-23`: Receipts pager buttons disable themselves under the focus, which drops to the page

- **Flags:** polish; size S
- **Screens:** `p.uploads.default.1280`, `p.uploads.viewer.1280`
- **Routes:** `/p/[practiceId]/uploads`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/components/uploads/receipts.tsx`
- **Evidence:** 'Newer' and 'Older' are buttons that become disabled on the first and last page (components/uploads/receipts.tsx:192-200); pressing 'Older' onto the last page disables the focused button, focus falls to <body>, and the new page is not announced. Only one page of receipts in the shots; code-read.
- **Rule:** WCAG 2.4.3 Focus Order (A); 4.1.3 Status Messages (AA)
- **Best fix:** Use aria-disabled (the button keeps focus) and announce 'Page 2 of 3, 50 files' in a polite status next to the pager.

### `a11y-practice-2-24`: The correspondence contact's fields carry no autocomplete purpose

- **Flags:** polish; size S
- **Screens:** `p.correspondence.default.1280`, `p.correspondence.error.1280`
- **Routes:** `/p/[practiceId]/settings/correspondence`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/settings/correspondence/page.tsx`
- **Evidence:** Name or office, phone, extension, email, street, suite, city and state have no autocomplete attribute; only ZIP has postal-code (settings/correspondence/page.tsx:73-86).
- **Rule:** WCAG 1.3.5 Identify Input Purpose (AA), where the contact is the administrator's own office details
- **Best fix:** autocomplete organization, tel-national, tel-extension, email, address-line1, address-line2, address-level2, address-level1 and postal-code.

## brand

### `brand-01`: Dark (tokens only): every sign-in step keeps the light Paper ground, so the white wordmark, the title and the links all but vanish

- **Flags:** should_fix; size S
- **Screens:** `sign-in.default.1280-dark`, `sign-in-enroll.default.1280-dark`, `sign-in-hardware-key.default.1280-dark`, `sign-in-mfa.default.1280-dark`, `sign-in-new-password.default.1280-dark`, `sign-in-register-key.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/sign-in`, `/sign-in/enroll`, `/sign-in/hardware-key`, `/sign-in/mfa`, `/sign-in/new-password`, `/sign-in/register-key`
- **Files:** `packages/brand/src/tokens.css`, `packages/brand/src/tailwind-theme.css`, `apps/web/src/app/sign-in/layout.tsx`, `tools/brand-colors/scan.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/brand.spec.ts`
- **Evidence:** With data-theme=dark the card, fields and button turn dark, but the ground stays cream (#F6F4EE). The ring and the wordmark turn white together (--pw-mark-ring), so on the cream ground the lockup shows only its brass keystone. The H1 ('Sign in', 'Verification code', 'Set up two-step verification', 'Choose a password', 'Register a hardware key') turns #F4F6FA. The footer links turn #8FB3F0 and the muted text #A7B1C2. Measured against Paper: wordmark 1.10:1, H1 1.02:1, links 1.93:1, muted text 1.97:1. Cause: apps/web/src/app/sign-in/layout.tsx:11 paints bg-paper, which reads --pw-paper (packages/brand/src/tailwind-theme.css:17), and the dark block (packages/brand/src/tokens.css:73-100) redefines no palette primitive, so --pw-paper has no dark value. The public, status and app layouts use bg-background and switch correctly (status.default.1280-dark).
- **Rule:** docs/brand/BRAND.md:84-86 (in dark mode the ground becomes a deeper ink and text near-white); BRAND.md:37 (the white ring belongs on dark grounds); .claude/rules/brand.md:39 (dark mode uses the semantic tokens); .claude/rules/brand.md:40 and WCAG 2.1 SC 1.4.3 (4.5:1). Advisory: dark (tokens only).
- **Best fix:** Give the sign-in ground a semantic token in place of the Paper primitive: --pw-bg-sign-in: var(--pw-paper) in :root and var(--pw-bg) under [data-theme=dark]/.dark in tokens.css, --color-bg-sign-in in tailwind-theme.css, and bg-bg-sign-in in sign-in/layout.tsx:11. Next to tools/brand-colors, add a static check that component code outside packages/brand and apps/web/src/components/brand uses no palette-primitive utility (bg-paper, bg-ink, text-ink, text-slate, border-line, bg-white), so no other surface can skip dark mode. Add the six sign-in steps to the dark pixel baselines in apps/web/e2e/brand.spec.ts.
- **Notes:** Dark (tokens only). Tokens with no dark value (tokens.css:7-70 against :73-100): --pw-paper causes the visible defect. --pw-ink, --pw-white, --pw-slate and --pw-line are palette primitives that tailwind-theme.css:15-20 exposes as bg-ink/text-ink/text-slate/border-line/bg-white; today only the reversed Lockup and Verified marks use text-white, and they do so on purpose. --pw-brass, --pw-accent and --pw-mark-keystone stay brass by design (BRAND.md:85). This becomes blocking the day dark mode reaches a user. Open brand decision: the sign-in ground in dark mode. Recommended: the dark ground (--pw-bg); Paper stays the light sign-in ground under ADR 0058 D9 item 1.

### `brand-02`: Native controls ignore the theme: no color-scheme and no accent-color, so dark fields keep dark-on-dark calendar and select glyphs, and both themes use the browser's blue for checked boxes and radios

- **Flags:** should_fix; size S
- **Screens:** `p.remittances.default.1280-dark`, `p.reports.default.1280-dark`, `p.roi.default.1280-dark`, `p.abstraction.default.1280-dark`, `p.contracts.default.1280-dark`, `p.pricing-runs.default.1280-dark`, `p.template.default.1280-dark`, `account.notifications.default.1280-dark`, `settings.users.default.1280-dark`, `p.packet.default.1280-dark`, `console.prospects.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/p/[practiceId]/remittances`, `/p/[practiceId]/reports`, `/p/[practiceId]/reports/roi`, `/p/[practiceId]/abstraction`, `/p/[practiceId]/contracts`, `/p/[practiceId]/pricing-runs`, `/p/[practiceId]/reconciliation/template`, `/account/notifications`, `/settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users`, `/p/[practiceId]/cases/[caseId]/packet`, `/console/prospects`
- **Files:** `packages/brand/src/tokens.css`, `apps/web/src/app/brand.css`, `apps/web/e2e/brand.spec.ts`
- **Evidence:** Dark: the calendar glyphs of the month and date inputs are dark on the navy field and nearly invisible. This affects 'Paid from/to' on Remittances, 'Month of service' on Reports, 'First/Last day' on ROI, 'Effective from/Last day' on Contracts and Abstraction, and 'First/Last month' on Pricing runs. The 'Choose a reason' select chevron is dark on dark. The 'They lose access now' and 'Attach to the evidence list' checkboxes render as white squares. In both themes, checked boxes and radios use Chrome's default blue (Notification preferences; the posting template's 'Our own…' radio), a colour that comes from no token. Cause: nothing sets color-scheme (a grep of apps/web/src, packages/brand/src and packages/ui/src finds none; tokens.css:7 and :73), and nothing sets accent-color (apps/web/src/app/brand.css; packages/ui/src/styles.css:68-81).
- **Rule:** .claude/rules/brand.md:13-16 (colours come from tokens); .claude/rules/brand.md:39 (dark mode through the semantic tokens); WCAG 2.1 SC 1.4.11 (non-text contrast of the calendar and select glyphs). The dark parts are advisory: dark (tokens only).
- **Best fix:** In tokens.css, set color-scheme: light on :root and color-scheme: dark in the [data-theme=dark]/.dark block, so every native glyph, picker and scrollbar follows the theme. In brand.css, set accent-color: var(--pw-primary) on input[type=checkbox], input[type=radio] and progress, so checked controls are Ink in light and near-white in dark. In apps/web/e2e/brand.spec.ts, check that the root's computed color-scheme and a checkbox's accent-color follow the theme.
- **Notes:** The light half (the browser's blue on checked controls) is live today on every form with a checkbox or radio: console status 'Components affected', notification preferences, users, packet evidence and the posting template. Open brand decision: the accent of native controls. Recommended: Ink (var(--pw-primary)), the colour of the primary action, never Brass (never a fill) or a status colour.

### `brand-03`: Dark (tokens only): printing a page in dark mode keeps the dark tokens, because the print block loses to the dark selector

- **Flags:** should_fix; size S
- **Screens:** `p.roi-statement.default.1280-dark`, `p.report.default.1280-dark`, `p.report-document.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/p/[practiceId]/reports/roi/[statementId]`, `/p/[practiceId]/reports/[reportId]`, `/p/[practiceId]/reports/documents/[documentId]`
- **Files:** `packages/brand/src/tokens.css`, `apps/web/e2e/brand.spec.ts`
- **Evidence:** This comes from the code; print was not captured. packages/brand/src/tokens.css:103-109 resets only --pw-bg, --pw-fg and --pw-mark-ring, under @media print { :root {…} }. That selector's specificity (0,1,0) is lower than :root[data-theme="dark"] (0,2,0) at tokens.css:73, so with data-theme=dark the dark values win even in print. A printed ROI statement or audit report would keep #F4F6FA text (1.08:1 on paper, since backgrounds do not print by default), #A7B1C2 muted text, the light-blue links and the dark status tints.
- **Rule:** packages/brand/src/tokens.css:102 (the print block exists for reports and statements); docs/brand/BRAND.md:68 (White is the report ground); WCAG 2.1 SC 1.4.3. Advisory: dark (tokens only).
- **Best fix:** In the print block, re-declare the whole light semantic set under ':root, :root[data-theme="dark"], .dark', so print is light whatever the screen theme: bg, bg-raised, bg-inset, fg, fg-muted, fg-inverse, border, border-strong, input-border, primary, primary-fg, link, focus, the six status tokens, danger-bg, mark-ring and color-scheme: light. In apps/web/e2e/brand.spec.ts, add a print check with page.emulateMedia({ media: 'print' }) and data-theme=dark on the ROI statement.
- **Notes:** Dark (tokens only). Reachable only once dark mode is switched on (brand-04).

### `brand-04`: Dark mode: recommend a per-user Appearance setting ('Match my device' by default, plus 'Light' and 'Dark'), switched on only after the dark set is fixed and checked

- **Flags:** should_fix; size M; needs a migration; touches #42
- **Screens:** `home.default.1280-dark`, `p.home.default.1280-dark`, `p.claims.default.1280-dark`, `status.default.1280-dark`, `sign-in.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/`, `/p/[practiceId]`, `/p/[practiceId]/claims`, `/status`, `/sign-in`
- **Files:** `packages/brand/src/tokens.css`, `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/account/notifications/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/account.ts`, `packages/db/src/schema/tenancy.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/brand.spec.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/a11y.ts`, `docs/adr/0056-wear-the-paidwhole-brand-from-one-token-source-self-hosted-fonts-and-status-as-text-on-tint.md`
- **Evidence:** Today no user reaches dark mode: nothing sets data-theme, and tokens.css has no prefers-color-scheme rule (ADR 0056:127-130). The dark set is close to ready: 76 of the 82 dark shots apply cleanly, and the six that do not are the sign-in steps. The measured dark contrasts all pass: text 16.8:1; muted text 8.4:1 on the ground and 7.7:1 on the rail; links 8.5:1; short 5.9:1, whole 6.2:1 and unpriced chip text 7.1:1 on their tints; field outlines 3.5:1 and 3.2:1; the brass keystone 6.5:1 on the rail. Three gaps remain: brand-01 (the sign-in ground), brand-02 (native controls) and brand-03 (print). The only dark check today is a screenshot that is saved and never compared (apps/web/e2e/brand.spec.ts:407-409), and the viewport's theme colour has one value (apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx:29).
- **Rule:** docs/brand/BRAND.md:84-86 defines dark mode. ADR 0056:127-130 and ADR 0058 D9 item 3 ('dark mode stays off', docs/product/reconciliation-001.md:309) keep it off until there is a setting. docs/product/PRD.md:256 (accessibility on every screen; no state that a refresh loses). The rules are silent on how a user reaches dark mode.
- **Best fix:** Recommended: a per-user Appearance setting with three choices: 'Match my device' (the default), 'Light' and 'Dark'. Store it on the user's server-side settings, so it follows the user across devices and survives a refresh. The server renders it into <html data-theme>, so the page never flashes the wrong theme. 'Match my device' works through a @media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) block in tokens.css scoped to :root:not([data-theme=light]). The public and sign-in pages have no user, so they follow the device. Print, the PDFs, the payer's copy and email stay light. The viewport's theme-color gets light and dark media variants. Gate the release: fix brand-01 to brand-03, add dark pixel baselines (the header, sign-in, a tile row, a status table) to brand.spec.ts, and run axe in both themes in the accessibility journeys (apps/web/e2e/a11y.ts) before the setting appears. Why this option: billing staff work long days in dense tables, and many set their OS to dark, some because of light sensitivity, so following the device honours a choice they have already made. The switch lets the same person force Light to share a screen with a payer or colleague, take a screenshot for support, or print, which following the device alone cannot do. Light-only would ignore that preference while the brand already defines a dark set that nearly passes. Rejected: following the device with no switch (no way to force Light for sharing or printing); light-only (ignores an OS-level preference and leaves BRAND.md's dark mode as dead code).
- **Notes:** This reverses a recorded owner ruling (reconciliation-001 D9 item 3, accepted in ADR 0058: dark mode stays off). Under the brief, that makes it the founder's call through the co-founder. Until the founder rules, ship brand-01 to brand-03 so the tokens are ready. The stored preference needs a migration: user.settings is jsonb with a database check that admits only the notifications key (packages/db/src/schema/tenancy.ts:60-73; migration 20261020090000). So the setting itself is a hand-off to Lane A ('Handed to Lane A'); the token, gate and baseline work stays in Lane B. Open brand decision: how users reach dark mode. Recommended as above.

### `brand-05`: Notices do not tell success, information, warning and danger apart: three kinds share one neutral look, the same warning appears in two tones, and every notice is announced as an alert

- **Flags:** should_fix; size M; touches #42
- **Screens:** `p.roi-statement.default.1280`, `request-audit.default.1280`, `support.default.1280`, `terms.default.1280`, `sign-in.default.1280`, `p.contract-version.default.1280-dark`, `p.packet.default.1280`
- **Routes:** `/p/[practiceId]/reports/roi/[statementId]`, `/request-audit`, `/support`, `/terms`, `/sign-in`, `/p/[practiceId]/contracts/versions/[versionId]`, `/p/[practiceId]/cases/[caseId]/packet`, `/p/[practiceId]/abstraction/regulations`, `/settings/billing/[organizationId]`, `/p/[practiceId]/cases/[caseId]`
- **Files:** `packages/ui/src/components/alert.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/components/components.test.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/action-form.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/packets/display.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/contracts/review.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/abstraction/regulations/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/settings/billing/[organizationId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/cases/[caseId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/cases/[caseId]/submissions/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/reports/roi/[statementId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/screening/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/screening/upload-form.tsx`
- **Evidence:** packages/ui/src/components/alert.tsx:6-14 has two variants. Default is bg-card, the same white bordered box as a Card. Destructive is a white box with only its title in danger, without the danger tint. Success notices look exactly like information. The success notices are at account/notifications/page.tsx:32, settings/correspondence/page.tsx:38, reconciliation/template/page.tsx:44, settings/pricing/page.tsx:51-61, contracts/versions/[versionId]/page.tsx:47, console/models/page.tsx:33 and components/billing/stripe-payment-method.tsx:81. The information notices include reports/roi/[statementId]/page.tsx:52 ('Recomputed from its entries', which on p.roi-statement.default.1280 reads as a small card), request-audit/page.tsx:37, terms/page.tsx:45 and support/page.tsx:34. Warnings split by screen. 'A payment failed' is default (settings/billing/[organizationId]/page.tsx:52), while 'Follow-up due' is destructive (cases/[caseId]/page.tsx:60, cases/[caseId]/submissions/page.tsx:158). The same Medicare Advantage dispute-route notice is destructive on the packet (components/packets/display.tsx:235) and default on Regulations (abstraction/regulations/page.tsx:58). The contract-window warning is a paragraph of danger text with no tint (components/contracts/review.tsx:106; visible on p.contract-version.default.1280-dark). Alert defaults to role='alert' (alert.tsx:18), so an information note interrupts a screen reader as an error does.
- **Rule:** Reviewer brief (success, warning and neutral notices must be told apart); docs/brand/BRAND.md:81-82 (colours that must be told apart also differ in lightness and always carry a label); ADR 0056:90-91 (a failure is danger text on the danger tint); .claude/rules/brand.md:26-28 (status colours are for data only, so a success notice cannot borrow Whole); WCAG 2.1 SC 4.1.3 (status messages) and 1.3.1.
- **Best fix:** Build one Notice in packages/ui with four kinds. Surface, icon and words tell them apart; no kind borrows a status colour. Info: the inset tint (bg-bg-inset) with its rule, an 'i' icon, role=note. Success: the ground with its rule, a check icon, a title that says what was done, role=status. Warning: the ground with a 3 px Ink left rule, a warning icon, a title that names the action needed, role=status. Danger: danger text and rule on the danger tint, an alert icon, role=alert, with focus moved to it as ActionForm already does. Map each of the roughly 80 Alert call sites to a kind by what it says. Add a unit test that every Alert passes a kind. The icons are inline SVG, aria-hidden, drawn once in packages/ui.
- **Notes:** Open brand decision: a warning hue. Recommended: none. The palette already holds terracotta Short and gold Brass, and an amber warning would sit between them in hue. A ruled neutral notice with an icon and words keeps the brand quiet ('everything else is quiet', BRAND.md:21) and needs no palette change, which only the brand owner may make (ADR 0058 D9). Open brand decision: the colour of success notices. Recommended: no status colour (Whole stays data only); a check icon, words and role=status.

### `brand-06`: The danger red, which shares Short's values, marks states that are not failures: 'To do', 'Missing', 'Open', 'Awaiting approval', 'Not built yet' and 'Action needed'

- **Flags:** should_fix; size S
- **Screens:** `p.settings.default.1280-dark`, `p.agreements.default.1280-dark`, `console.compliance.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/p/[practiceId]/settings`, `/p/[practiceId]/settings/agreements`, `/console/compliance`, `/p/[practiceId]/contracts/versions/[versionId]`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/settings/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/settings/agreements/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/compliance/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/contracts/display.tsx`
- **Evidence:** The practice's Getting started checklist shows three red 'To do' badges (settings/page.tsx:56). The agreement chain shows red 'Missing' badges for the terms and the data-rights addendum (settings/agreements/page.tsx:47). The SOC 2 page puts red 'Action needed', 'Partly built', 'Awaiting approval' and 'Not built yet' badges on its controls and evidence items (console/compliance/page.tsx:72, :125, :167). An open question to the customer is red (components/contracts/display.tsx:146). All of these use Badge variant destructive, which is --pw-danger text on --pw-danger-bg, and both tokens read Short's values (packages/brand/src/tokens.css:43-46). On a payment product, that terracotta means 'paid below the contract'.
- **Rule:** .claude/rules/brand.md:26-28 (status colours are for data only); ADR 0056:90-92 (danger is for a failure: a failed run, a refused check, an evidence gap; it is named apart from Short although both still read Short's values).
- **Best fix:** Keep the danger tone for failures and refusals only: a failed job or invoice, a refused export or check, a suspended account, a failed verifier. Show pending and to-do states as the neutral chip with words (To do, Missing, Open, Awaiting approval, Not built yet, Action needed). Carry urgency in words and order (to-dos first), not in colour. Add a test that Badge variant destructive and StatusChip tone danger appear only beside a failure state.
- **Notes:** Open brand decision: whether danger gets a red of its own, apart from Short (ADR 0056 named the token apart; tokens.css:44-46). Recommended: no new hue. Restrict where danger appears instead, so it never meets a payment figure.

### `brand-07`: System-state badges come in three looks (filled, outline, borderless) and two radii, so the same kind of state looks different from screen to screen

- **Flags:** should_fix; size M; touches #42
- **Screens:** `status.default.1280`, `p.report.default.1280`, `console.organizations.default.1280`, `console.status.default.1280`, `p.packet.default.1280`, `p.settings.default.1280-dark`, `account.devices.default.1280-dark`, `console.billing.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/status`, `/p/[practiceId]/reports/[reportId]`, `/console/organizations`, `/console/status`, `/p/[practiceId]/cases/[caseId]/packet`, `/p/[practiceId]/settings`, `/account/devices`, `/console/billing`, `/console/support`
- **Files:** `packages/ui/src/components/badge.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/status.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/status/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/support/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/compliance/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/settings/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/contracts/review.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/contracts/display.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/uploads/receipts.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/account/devices/page.tsx`
- **Evidence:** ADR 0056 gives a system state one look: Ink text on the inset tint with its rule (Badge default, packages/ui/src/components/badge.tsx:13). In use, 'Ready' and 'Released' on the audit report are filled (p.report.default.1280). These are outline, white with a rule: 'Operational' on the public status page (status/page.tsx:39), 'active' on the console, 'Done' on Settings (settings/page.tsx:56) and the 'Good' standing on billing. These are borderless (secondary): 'Not checked' (components/contracts/review.tsx:229) and 'This device' (account/devices/page.tsx:37). An open support grant is filled and a closed one is outline (app/p/[practiceId]/page.tsx:480; console/support/page.tsx:85). Badge is rounded-md (badge.tsx:7), while StatusChip has the identical neutral tone at rounded-sm (components/status.tsx:19, :25). On the packet, the 'Current' badge and the 'Deadline verified' chip sit side by side in different shapes. Across 90 Badge uses, the variant is chosen 30 different ways.
- **Rule:** ADR 0056:89-90 (a system state is a neutral badge: Ink text on the inset tint with its rule); apps/web/src/app/brand.css:43-49 (status pills: radius-sm, 12 px, 500); reviewer brief (badges look and behave the same across screens).
- **Best fix:** Use one chip component for every state (apps/web/src/components/status.tsx, or moved into packages/ui) with the tones neutral, whole, short, unpriced and danger, radius-sm, 12 px, weight 500. Replace Badge wherever it shows a state. Keep Badge outline only for tags that are not states (admin, Proposed by the model, an organization's kind). Add a test that fails on Badge variant secondary or outline next to a state word.
- **Notes:** Open brand decision: chip shape. Recommended: one chip at radius-sm (4 px), as brand.css already sets for .pw-status-*.

### `brand-08`: Figure tiles are drawn five ways, and the audit report's headline breaks the brand's tile rule with link-blue values and uppercase labels

- **Flags:** should_fix; size M
- **Screens:** `p.report.default.1280`, `p.report.default.1280-dark`, `p.report-review.default.1280-dark`, `p.home.default.1280`, `screening-result.default.1280`, `console.specialist-queue.default.1280-dark`, `p.reconciliation.default.1280-dark`, `p.compare.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/p/[practiceId]/reports/[reportId]`, `/p/[practiceId]/reports/[reportId]/review`, `/p/[practiceId]`, `/screening/results/[resultId]`, `/console/specialist-queue`, `/p/[practiceId]/reconciliation`, `/p/[practiceId]/pricing-runs/compare`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/components/reports/display.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/screening/result-summary.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/specialist-queue/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/reconciliation/display.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/pricing-runs/compare/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/reconciliation/page.tsx`, `apps/web/e2e/brand.spec.ts`
- **Evidence:** The practice home's tiles follow BRAND.md: label in Slate 12 px sentence case, value in Plex Mono 500 in Ink (app/p/[practiceId]/page.tsx:20-23). The audit report's headline (components/reports/display.tsx:82-86) breaks it. Its labels are uppercase, semibold and tracked. Its values are text-2xl font-semibold inside an <a> with no text colour, so brand.css:27 (a { color: var(--pw-link) }) paints '$90.00' link blue on p.report.default.1280, and light blue in dark. Four other patterns: screening stats have a 12 px label and a text-lg semibold value (components/screening/result-summary.tsx:106-112); the specialist queue sets a 16 px CardDescription label over a 30 px bold CardTitle figure (console/specialist-queue/page.tsx:38-62); the reconciliation totals use a text-lg weight-500 value (components/reconciliation/display.tsx:89-104); the run comparison and the match lists use uppercase semibold labels (pricing-runs/compare/page.tsx:31; reconciliation/page.tsx:92, :338, :470).
- **Rule:** docs/brand/BRAND.md:125-126 and ADR 0056:47-48 (tiles: label in Slate 12 px, value in Plex Mono 500, note in Slate); BRAND.md:94 (totals at 500); BRAND.md:5-6 (nothing brand-related hard-coded outside the brand's components).
- **Best fix:** Build one StatTile (label, value, note, optional href) beside the brand components. Label: Slate, 12 px, weight 500, sentence case. Value: Plex Mono 500 with tabular figures, in the foreground colour, at one size. Note: Slate, 12 px. A tile that links keeps the foreground colour with a hover surface, as the practice home's does. Replace the five patterns with it. Extend apps/web/e2e/brand.spec.ts:347 to check the report headline's value colour and weight, as it already checks the dashboard's.
- **Notes:** Open brand decision: tile label case. Recommended: sentence case, as BRAND.md section 7 and the practice home have it. Uppercase is not in the brand's type table. The specialist queue's 30 px values also share brand-09's synthetic bold.

### `brand-09`: Figures are set in Plex Mono weights the brand does not ship (600 and 700), so the browser fakes the bold

- **Flags:** should_fix; size S; touches #42
- **Screens:** `screening-result.default.1280`, `p.roi-statement.default.1280`, `p.report.default.1280`, `console.specialist-queue.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/screening/results/[resultId]`, `/p/[practiceId]/reports/roi/[statementId]`, `/p/[practiceId]/reports/[reportId]`, `/console/specialist-queue`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/components/remittances/display.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/monitoring/display.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/screening/result-summary.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/reports/display.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/reports/roi.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/specialist-queue/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/type-scale.test.ts`
- **Evidence:** apps/web/src/lib/fonts.ts:36-44 loads IBM Plex Mono at 400 and 500 only, yet several figures inherit heavier weights. The report headline's value is font-semibold (components/reports/display.tsx:84). The screening stats are font-semibold (components/screening/result-summary.tsx:110); on the result, '$11,716.59' and the other stats read heavy and smeared. Every CardTitle that holds a figure inherits font-bold from packages/ui/src/components/card.tsx:21: the ROI cards' 'Recovered principal, net (deposited): $12.00' (components/reports/roi.tsx:33-35) and the specialist queue's counts (console/specialist-queue/page.tsx:41-62). The browser synthesizes those weights, so the most important figures show a smeared imitation of the brand's mono.
- **Rule:** docs/brand/BRAND.md:94 (money and tables: IBM Plex Mono 400 and 500; totals 500); .claude/rules/brand.md:36.
- **Best fix:** Make Money, and each mono figure component, set font-medium (500) explicitly, so a heading's or a tile's weight never reaches the figure: components/remittances/display.tsx:18 and its screening and monitoring twins. Remove font-semibold and font-bold from figure elements. Extend apps/web/src/app/type-scale.test.ts to fail on font-mono beside font-semibold or font-bold.

### `brand-10`: Dollar amounts inside sentences render in Plex Sans: the digest, the contract terms, the materiality line and the screening's upload messages

- **Flags:** should_fix; size M; touches #42
- **Screens:** `p.digest.default.1280-dark`, `p.contract-version.default.1280-dark`, `p.defaults.default.1280-dark`, `p.roi-statement.default.1280`
- **Routes:** `/p/[practiceId]/digests/[digestId]`, `/p/[practiceId]/contracts/versions/[versionId]`, `/p/[practiceId]/settings/defaults`, `/p/[practiceId]/reports/roi/[statementId]`, `/screening`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/digests/[digestId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/contracts/display.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/contracts/review.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/contracts/review.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/settings/defaults/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/screening/messages.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/reports/roi/[statementId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/e2e/brand.spec.ts`
- **Evidence:** The digest page prints the email's sentences as plain strings (app/p/[practiceId]/digests/[digestId]/page.tsx:38-39), so '$90.00 on 3 lines' and '$450.00 of $450.00 billed (100.0%)' are set in Plex Sans (p.digest.default.1280-dark). Contract terms arrive as strings from lib/contracts/display.ts:151-179, through lib/contracts/review.ts:74, and print in a table cell (components/contracts/review.tsx:247): the rate '110% of Medicare' is in Plex Sans on p.contract-version.default.1280-dark, and a fixed schedule's '$50.00 per unit' is built the same way (display.ts:151, :158). The materiality line 'Now $500 per line and $25000 per case' is in sans (p.defaults dark; the wrong amounts are another lens's finding). The screening upload form's left-out and outside-content sentences build amounts into text with formatUsd (lib/screening/messages.ts:134, :170; from the code, not in this set). The ROI statement's 'Pursuit hours logged: 0.00' sets a quantity in the display face (reports/roi/[statementId]/page.tsx:142).
- **Rule:** .claude/rules/brand.md:36-37 (every money figure, code, unit count and rate in the mono font with tabular numerals); ADR 0056:50-51 (running text and button words included, through <Money/>).
- **Best fix:** Have the sentence builders return parts (text plus cents, counts and rates) instead of finished strings, so the pages render <Money/> and mono counts. Where a string must stay (the email shares the digest's sentences), render it through a <Figures> component, the web twin of the PDF's escapeWithFigures (ADR 0056:108-109), which sets each dollar amount, percent, rate and count in the mono face with tabular numerals. Bring the PDF's check to the web: an e2e step lays out the digest, contract-version, defaults and screening pages and finds no '$' amount outside IBM Plex Mono.

### `brand-11`: The screening result colours an investigatory anomaly in Short, the 'paid below the contract' colour, on a page that says it makes no finding about a payer

- **Flags:** should_fix; size S
- **Screens:** `screening-result.default.1280`, `screening-result.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/screening/results/[resultId]`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/components/screening/anomalies.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/screening/result.test.tsx`
- **Evidence:** On the public screening result, the anomaly row's chip is Short's terracotta on its tint: 'Anomaly: more than $5 a claim line below…'. The 'others' row turns Short when any group is an anomaly (components/screening/anomalies.tsx:151-157, :262). The same page says 'A screening is investigatory… it is not a contract figure, and nothing here is a finding about a payer'. The coverage table on the same page deliberately keeps whole and short off the screening (components/screening/coverage.tsx:26-33: 'whole means paid in full, which a screening never says') and uses unpriced and neutral tones.
- **Rule:** ADR 0056:80 (Flagged (investigatory) takes the unpriced tone: a line with no contract price, short of its cohort, never a claim); .claude/rules/brand.md:26-28; ADR 0058 (a screening never says owed or underpaid; the copy lint guards the words, but the Short colour says it anyway).
- **Best fix:** Show the anomaly in the unpriced tone (Slate text, unpriced rule) and the other standings in neutral, words unchanged. Add a unit test that no screening component uses the short or whole tone.
- **Notes:** The anomalies table's clipping at 1280 px is the earlier tour's item and is not repeated here.

### `brand-12`: Page headers have no single rhythm: a help-link band sits above every title, and ledes come at two sizes and two distances

- **Flags:** polish; size S; touches #42
- **Screens:** `account.devices.default.1280-dark`, `account.notifications.default.1280-dark`, `settings.billing.default.1280-dark`, `status.default.1280`, `p.access-log.default.1280-dark`, `p.uploads.default.1280-dark`, `console.status.default.1280`
- **Routes:** `/account/devices`, `/account/notifications`, `/settings/billing`, `/status`, `/maintenance`, `/p/[practiceId]/settings/access-log`, `/p/[practiceId]/uploads`, `/console/status`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/account/devices/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/account/notifications/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/settings/billing/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/status/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/maintenance/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/settings/access-log/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/type-scale.test.ts`
- **Evidence:** 48 pages set the lede at 16 px (text-base text-muted-foreground) in a gap-1 group under the H1. Devices, Notification preferences and Billing set a 14 px max-w-prose lede outside that group, 24 px below the title (account/devices/page.tsx:23-26, account/notifications/page.tsx:28, settings/billing/page.tsx:27): the lede starts at y 201 there, against y 183 on Uploads, under the same H1 at y 150. The public status line is 14 px under a 32 px title (status/page.tsx:25), and so is the maintenance line (maintenance/page.tsx:18). The access log's lede is 14 px under the settings tabs (settings/access-log/page.tsx:31). Every shell page opens with a 40 px band that holds only the right-aligned 'Help with this screen' (components/shell.tsx:335-349), while console status puts its 'Public status page' link on the title row (console.status.default.1280). h3 is styled five ways (font-semibold, font-medium, text-sm font-semibold, text-base font-semibold, and the card title).
- **Rule:** ADR 0056:48-50 (one type scale: page title 32/700, section 20/700, card title 18/700, running text such as a page's lede 16/24, tables 14); docs/brand/BRAND.md:92 (16/24 body).
- **Best fix:** Build a PageHeader (breadcrumb; H1 with its state chips; a 16/24 Slate lede; a right-hand slot for the page's actions, the help disclosure and, where earned, the seal) and use it on every shell and public page. Moving 'Help with this screen' into that slot lets the title start at the top of the content. Extend apps/web/src/app/type-scale.test.ts so a page's lede must be the PageHeader's.

### `brand-13`: The home page's title is the brand name, a second live-text 'PaidWhole' at the wordmark's face and weight but not its tracking, directly under the header lockup

- **Flags:** polish; size S
- **Screens:** `home.default.1280`, `home.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/app/page.tsx`
- **Evidence:** apps/web/src/app/page.tsx:28 sets the H1 'PaidWhole' at 32 px in Schibsted Grotesk 700 with -0.02em tracking, directly under the header's lockup (home.default.1280 and dark). It reads as a second, off-spec wordmark: the wordmark's face and weight, but not its -0.03em tracking. It also names nothing about the screen; the empty state below says 'No practice to open'.
- **Rule:** .claude/rules/brand.md:20-22 (the wordmark is one word in the display face at 700 and -0.03em, never another tracking); docs/brand/BRAND.md:3-6 (nothing brand-related is hand-drawn or hard-coded outside the brand's components).
- **Best fix:** Title the screen for what it shows (for example 'Your workspace', with the empty state's 'No practice to open' below it) and leave the name to the lockup. The words lens picks the words.
- **Notes:** Open brand decision: whether a page title may be the bare brand name. Recommended: no; the lockup carries the name, and titles name screens.

### `brand-14`: Public pages each assemble their own frame: four content widths, five different footer sentences, and a header with no visible way to sign in

- **Flags:** polish; size S; touches #42
- **Screens:** `request-audit.default.1280`, `screening.default.1280`, `screening-result.default.1280`, `status.default.1280`, `security.default.1280-dark`, `maintenance.default.1280-dark`, `sign-in.default.1280`
- **Routes:** `/request-audit`, `/screening`, `/screening/results/[resultId]`, `/status`, `/security`, `/maintenance`, `/sign-in`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/components/public-header.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/request-audit/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/screening/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/screening/result-view.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/status/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/security/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/maintenance/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/not-found.tsx`
- **Evidence:** Each public page places PublicHeader and its own <main>. The widths: request-audit max-w-xl (request-audit/page.tsx:29); screening, status and security max-w-3xl (screening/page.tsx:34, status/page.tsx:22, security/page.tsx:29); the screening result max-w-5xl (components/screening/result-view.tsx:21); maintenance max-w-xl (maintenance/page.tsx:16); sign-in max-w-md (sign-in/page.tsx:17). Each page closes with its own sentence of links, and the five sentences differ: 'Not ready for an audit? Try a free screening…' (request-audit), 'Want your lines priced…? Request an audit' (screening), 'Questions: support@… Sign in' (status), 'Service status: status page. Customers: sign in.' (security), 'Screen another file: free screening' (result). The header holds the lockup alone, linked to /sign-in, with no visible 'Sign in' (components/public-header.tsx:6-13). In request-audit's narrow column the 32 px title breaks inside the compound: 'contract-' / 'payment audit'.
- **Rule:** Reviewer brief (consistency between the practice, console, public and sign-in layouts). docs/brand/BRAND.md section 7 and ADR 0056 are silent on the public frame.
- **Best fix:** Build a PublicLayout with three parts. The header: the lockup on the left and a 'Sign in' link on the right. One content width for reading pages (max-w-3xl), with the wide width only for the screening result's tables. One shared footer in Slate 14 px: Request an audit, Free screening, Service status, Security, Sign in and the support address. Each page keeps only its own call to action. Sign-in keeps its own Paper frame, as ADR 0058 D9 item 1 decided.
- **Notes:** Open brand decision: the public frame. Recommended: one PublicLayout and footer as above; the screening keeps the lockup without the tagline (ADR 0058, D11).

### `brand-15`: Navigation is drawn two ways: the rail's links are in the text colour with the brass underline, while the settings tabs are link-blue pills with 10 px 'admin' tags

- **Flags:** polish; size S; touches #42
- **Screens:** `p.settings.default.1280-dark`, `p.agreements.default.1280-dark`, `p.access-log.default.1280-dark`, `p.correspondence.default.1280-dark`, `p.defaults.default.1280-dark`, `p.pricing-settings.default.1280-dark`, `p.data.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/p/[practiceId]/settings`, `/p/[practiceId]/settings/agreements`, `/p/[practiceId]/settings/access-log`, `/p/[practiceId]/settings/correspondence`, `/p/[practiceId]/settings/defaults`, `/p/[practiceId]/settings/pricing`, `/p/[practiceId]/settings/data`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/components/settings/nav.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx`
- **Evidence:** Rail links are in the text colour; the active one gets the 2 px brass underline and weight 500 (components/shell.tsx:112-124). The settings section tabs are bordered pills whose text is link blue, because they are <a> elements with no text colour (components/settings/nav.tsx:37-41; brand.css:27). The active tab gets the inset fill and weight 500, and the tabs carry 10 px 'admin' badges (nav.tsx:45, text-[10px]). The dark shots show the tab labels in light blue next to the white rail. The brass underline is 2.4:1 against the rail's surface. It is decorative beside aria-current and the weight change, but the weight change from 400 to 500 is a faint cue.
- **Rule:** docs/brand/BRAND.md:120-121 (one brass underline marks the active navigation item; that is Brass's only job in the UI); ADR 0056:46 and 48-50 (the type scale has no 10 px size). The rules are silent on sub-navigation.
- **Best fix:** Set the settings tabs in the foreground colour (text-fg, no underline); mark the active tab with the inset fill, weight 500 and aria-current, and no brass, because the brand gives brass to one active underline per screen and the rail already carries it. Show 'admin' as a 12 px neutral tag, or as the word 'administrator' in Slate. On the rail, give the active item the inset fill too, so its state does not rest on the 2.4:1 underline and a weight change.
- **Notes:** Open brand decision: how sub-navigation shows its active item. Recommended: the inset fill with text in the foreground colour, never brass and never link blue.

### `brand-16`: The UI's greys and interaction colours sit outside BRAND.md's palette and mix warm and cool, and the primary button's hover is imperceptible

- **Flags:** polish; size S
- **Screens:** `console.organizations.default.1280`, `p.home.default.1280`, `request-audit.default.1280`, `console.status.default.1280`
- **Routes:** `/console/organizations`, `/p/[practiceId]`, `/request-audit`, `/console/status`
- **Files:** `packages/brand/src/tokens.css`, `packages/brand/src/tokens.ts`, `docs/brand/BRAND.md`
- **Evidence:** tokens.css:26-27, :32 and :34 add cool greys (#F7F8FA raised, #EFF1F4 inset, #B9BEC8 border-strong, #808996 input-border) beside the brand's warm Line (#DAD7CF, used for card and table rules) and warm Paper. tokens.css:39-42 add link #1F4E9A, focus #2F5DA8 and primary-hover #15284A. BRAND.md:64-71 lists none of these; ADR 0058 asked the brand owner only about input-border and danger-bg. On console.organizations.default.1280 the beige table rules and card border sit beside the cool-grey rail and badge fills. primary-hover is 1.13:1 against Ink (1.08:1 in dark, #FFFFFF against #F4F6FA), so a hovered primary button does not visibly change.
- **Rule:** docs/brand/BRAND.md:59-71 (the palette); ADR 0058 D9 (the brand owner alone changes the palette).
- **Best fix:** Send this to the brand owner with these recommendations: adopt link, focus and the surface tokens into BRAND.md section 4; retune raised, inset, border-strong and input-border into the warm family of Line and Paper, keeping input-border at 3:1 or more on White, Paper and the raised surface; give primary-hover a visible step in both themes (1.5:1 or more against the resting fill).
- **Notes:** Brand-owner decision (ADR 0058 D9); the colours change in tokens only. Passes noted: no raw colour in component code (only token utilities, plus opacity modifiers on tokens such as bg-destructive/20; tools/brand-colors holds hex), brass is never text (only decoration-brass at shell.tsx:119 and the keystone), and the mark and lockup render correctly in every light shot and every dark shot except sign-in. The brand decisions the rules leave open, each with the recommended option: (1) how users reach dark mode: a per-user setting, 'Match my device' by default (brand-04; the founder's ruling, since it reverses D9 item 3); (2) the sign-in ground in dark: the dark ground (brand-01); (3) a warning hue: none, a ruled neutral notice with icon and words (brand-05); (4) the colour of success notices: none from the status set, a check icon and words with role=status (brand-05); (5) a danger red apart from Short: no new hue, danger only on failures (brand-06); (6) link, focus and surfaces: adopt into BRAND.md section 4, warm neutrals, a visible hover step (this finding); (7) chip shape: one chip at radius-sm, 12 px, 500 (brand-07); (8) tile label case: sentence case (brand-08); (9) sub-navigation active state: inset fill, foreground text, no brass (brand-15); (10) which actions take the danger style: those that remove access, remove or reverse a record, or write money off (brand-18); (11) the public frame: one PublicLayout and footer (brand-14); (12) the seal's place on a web page: the page header's right slot (brand-17); (13) 'Verified' on chips beyond rate records ('Deadline verified', apps/web/src/lib/packets/view.ts:95): keep 'Verified' for the ledger-gated seal and badge as ADR 0056 does for rate records, and show the deadline status in other words with the CO-12 enum unchanged (the words lens picks the words); (14) --pw-unpriced, still open with the brand owner (D9 item 2): #5F6A79 (4.9:1 on its tint, 5.5:1 on White), after which unpriced chips may set their text in their own hue; (15) the accent of native controls: Ink (brand-02); (16) a page title that is the bare brand name: no (brand-13).

### `brand-17`: On the ROI statement the Verified seal floats alone at the right of an empty band, cut off from the title and the figure it attests

- **Flags:** polish; size S; in flight: #46
- **Screens:** `p.roi-statement.default.1280`, `p.roi-statement.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/p/[practiceId]/reports/roi/[statementId]`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/reports/roi/[statementId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/brand/Verified.tsx`
- **Evidence:** The seal and its definition sit alone, right-aligned (reports/roi/[statementId]/page.tsx:49, className self-end), in a band about 170 px tall whose left two-thirds are empty, above the 'Recomputed from its entries' notice and away from the Recovered card it attests (light and dark).
- **Rule:** docs/brand/BRAND.md:111-114 (the definition renders with the seal on the same page: met); BRAND.md:140 (on a report cover, the seal sits bottom right when earned). The brand is silent on where the seal goes on a web page.
- **Best fix:** Put the seal, with its definition under it, in the right-hand slot of the page header (brand-12's PageHeader), beside the title and lede, so it reads as the statement's mark and leaves no empty band.

### `brand-18`: The danger style on actions follows no rule: 'End membership' is red on one screen and plain on another, and comparable removals are outline

- **Flags:** polish; size S; touches #42
- **Screens:** `settings.users.default.1280-dark`, `console.organization.default.1280-dark`, `account.devices.default.1280-dark`, `console.support.default.1280-dark`
- **Routes:** `/settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users`, `/console/organizations/[organizationId]`, `/account/devices`, `/console/support`, `/p/[practiceId]/abstraction/versions/[versionId]`, `/p/[practiceId]/cases/[caseId]`, `/p/[practiceId]/reconciliation`
- **Files:** `packages/ui/src/components/button.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/organizations/[organizationId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/account/devices/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/support/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/p/[practiceId]/abstraction/versions/[versionId]/page.tsx`
- **Evidence:** 'End membership' is a destructive (danger) button on Users and roles (settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users/page.tsx:80; the red outlined buttons in settings.users dark) but an outline button on the console's organization page (console/organizations/[organizationId]/page.tsx:162). These are outline too: 'Revoke this device and sign out' (account/devices/page.tsx:46), 'End now' on a support grant (console/support/page.tsx:99), and 'Remove this record' and 'Withdraw the question' (abstraction/versions/[versionId]/page.tsx:163, :474). Meanwhile 'Write off', 'Reverse the match' and 'Adjust the amount' are destructive (cases/[caseId]/page.tsx:202, reconciliation/page.tsx:36, abstraction/ledger/page.tsx:25).
- **Rule:** Reviewer brief (buttons look and behave the same across screens). The rules are silent on which actions take the danger style; packages/ui/src/components/button.tsx:13-15 defines it.
- **Best fix:** Write one rule in the Button's comment and enforce it with a test: an action that removes access, removes or reverses a record, or writes money off takes the destructive variant; everything else is default or outline. Apply it to the call sites above. Whether each action also needs a confirm step is the flows lens's question.
- **Notes:** Open brand decision: which actions take the danger style. Recommended: those that remove access, remove or reverse a record, or write money off.

## flows-other

### `flows-other-01`: Every ActionForm empties all its fields when the server refuses it, so a person retypes the whole form

- **Flags:** blocking; size S
- **Screens:** `request-audit.default.1280`, `request-audit.attribution.1280`, `screening-result.default.1280`, `support.default.1280`, `sign-in.default.1280`, `console.organizations.default.1280`, `console.practice.default.1280`, `settings.users.default.1280`
- **Routes:** `/request-audit`, `/screening/results/[resultId]`, `/support`, `/sign-in`, `/console/organizations`, `/console/practices/[practiceId]`, `/settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/components/action-form.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/action-form.test.tsx`, `apps/web/e2e/onboarding.spec.ts`
- **Evidence:** ActionForm posts through <form action={formAction}> (apps/web/src/components/action-form.tsx:65). Under React 19.3 (pnpm-workspace.yaml:20-21) a form whose action is a function has its uncontrolled fields reset when the action settles, whatever it returns, and a refusal returns normally (apps/web/src/lib/http/next.ts:161-174). ActionState carries no submitted values (next.ts:124-129), and Field, SelectField and TextAreaField take no value from it (apps/web/src/components/field.tsx:36,72). So after any refusal (a field screened as patient-like, a rate limit, 'Check the fields marked below', a CSRF reload message), the error summary says 'Check the fields marked below' over fields that are now empty: the request-audit form's eight answers (apps/web/src/app/request-audit/page.tsx:53-62), the screening result's convert form (apps/web/src/components/screening/convert-form.tsx:57-63, whose route refuses locations and payers server-side, apps/web/src/lib/screening/routes.ts:1180-1198), the support ticket's message (apps/web/src/app/support/page.tsx:49-55), the sign-in email, the console's new organization and practice settings (console/practices/[practiceId]/page.tsx:72-107, which fall back to the stored values, silently discarding the edit). The e2e journeys refill every field after each refusal (apps/web/e2e/helpers.ts:29-33 used by sign-in.spec.ts:56; onboarding.spec.ts:293-295), so no test notices. The screening upload form already avoids it by submitting from onSubmit with preventDefault and startTransition (apps/web/src/components/screening/upload-form.tsx:117-147,159), so two forms on the same prospect path behave differently.
- **Rule:** WCAG 2.2 SC 3.3.7 Redundant Entry (combined audit #103 adds WCAG 2.2); docs/product/PRD.md:256 (every error shown with a next step: the next step cannot be 'type it all again')
- **Best fix:** Make ActionForm keep what the person typed: submit from onSubmit with event.preventDefault() and startTransition(() => formAction(new FormData(form))), as the screening upload form does, so React never resets the form; clear only the secret fields on purpose (password, six-digit code), and say so under them. Keep the per-field errors and focus handling as they are. Add a component test (refuse once, the fields still hold their values; a password field is empty) and an e2e step on /request-audit that submits a payer name shaped like an identifier and asserts every other field kept its value.
- **Notes:** Cannot be shown in a still image without a refusal shot; verified from the code and React 19's documented form-action reset. 60 files use ActionForm, so one change fixes every form in the app. #48 (narrative survives sign-out) is a different loss and stays in flight.

### `flows-other-02`: No way back in for a person who forgets a password or loses an authenticator or hardware key: sign-in is a dead end

- **Flags:** blocking; size L; touches #42
- **Screens:** `sign-in.default.1280`, `sign-in.reason-idle.1280`, `sign-in-mfa.default.1280`, `sign-in-hardware-key.default.1280`, `account.devices.default.1280`, `settings.users.default.1280`
- **Routes:** `/sign-in`, `/sign-in/mfa`, `/sign-in/hardware-key`, `/account/devices`, `/settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/app/sign-in/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/sign-in/mfa/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/sign-in/hardware-key/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/sign-in/notice.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/sign-in.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/auth/dev-store.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/account/security/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/account.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/settings.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx`
- **Evidence:** The sign-in card offers Continue, a hardware-key link, 'Request an audit' and 'Check the service status' (apps/web/src/app/sign-in/page.tsx:26-48): no 'Forgot your password?', and the code step has no 'Can't use your authenticator?' (apps/web/src/app/sign-in/mfa/page.tsx:12-29). Nothing in apps/web/src or packages implements a password reset or a second-factor reset (no ForgotPassword, AdminResetUserPassword or TOTP reset anywhere). The locked notice says 'A platform administrator can unlock it.' (apps/web/src/app/sign-in/notice.tsx:17) but the sign-in pages never show how to reach one: the support address appears on /status and /maintenance, not on /sign-in, and /support needs a session. The organization's administrator cannot see that a member is locked or help them (the Users and roles badge shows only active/invited/no MFA, apps/web/src/app/settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users/page.tsx:69); only the console has Unlock (apps/web/src/app/console/organizations/[organizationId]/page.tsx:154-159), and it has no reset of a password or a second factor either. The account area has Devices, Notification preferences, Billing and Help (apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx:281-293) but no page to change a password, move the authenticator to a new phone, or add a backup hardware key, so a lost phone or key locks a person out for good.
- **Rule:** docs/product/PRD.md:256 (every error shown with a next step); PRD.md:266 (Sign-in and MFA: lockout after ten failures implies an unlock path the person can reach)
- **Best fix:** Give every sign-in step a way forward. (1) /sign-in: 'Forgot your password?' starting the identity provider's reset by email (Cognito ForgotPassword/ConfirmForgotPassword; the local store's equivalent), which still asks for the second factor afterwards. (2) /sign-in/mfa and /sign-in/hardware-key: 'Can't use your authenticator / key?' explaining the recovery: the organization's administrator (for customers) or a platform administrator (for staff) resets the second factor after checking who is asking, with a fresh second factor of their own, an audit event and an email to the person; the person enrols again at the next sign-in. (3) Users and roles shows 'locked' and offers Unlock and 'Reset two-step verification' to the organization's administrator. (4) A 'Sign-in and security' account page: change password, replace the authenticator (current code first), and register a second hardware key for staff. (5) The sign-in pages' trouble line names the support address, which opens a ticket from the account's own address (docs/runbooks/support-mail.md).
- **Notes:** A reset of the second factor by an administrator is a security design choice (who may vouch, with which proof); it needs the security reviewer and probably an ADR. The lockout itself (ten failures) is unchanged. touches42 because shell.tsx and routes/settings.ts are in #42; the sign-in part alone does not touch #42's files.

### `flows-other-03`: /console has no page and the internal roles land on a directory, a create form or a dead end instead of what needs them

- **Flags:** should_fix; size M; touches #42
- **Screens:** `console.organizations.default.1280`, `console.organizations.help.1280`, `home.default.1280`, `home.help.1280`, `home.account-menu.1280`, `console.prospects.default.1280`, `console.tickets.default.1280`, `console.specialist-queue.default.1280`
- **Routes:** `/console/organizations`, `/`, `/console`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/app/console/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/console.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/help.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/console/organizations/page.tsx`
- **Evidence:** There is no apps/web/src/app/console/page.tsx, so /console answers the root 404, and the rail's 'Internal console' is a label, not a link (apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx:232). '/' sends a platform administrator to /console/organizations (apps/web/src/app/page.tsx:23-25), whose first thing is the 'New organization' form above the directory (apps/web/src/app/console/organizations/page.tsx:34-45): the operator's most rare act leads, and nothing says that two audit requests are new, a ticket is open, 13 support grants are running, maintenance is on, a component is degraded, an organization is past due or a release draft waits; each lives on its own rail page with no count. A support engineer without a grant stays on '/' and sees H1 'PaidWhole' and the empty state 'No practice to open ... support access comes from a ticket. Ask your administrator.' (apps/web/src/app/page.tsx:26-34; shot home.default.1280) although their work is the ticket queue one rail link away; its help text tells them to 'pick a practice in the switcher, or open the console' (apps/web/src/components/help.ts:9) and they have neither. The route map records that a reviewer who holds a grant is redirected to a practice home they are forbidden to open (route-map.json, '/' notes; apps/web/src/app/page.tsx:19-22).
- **Rule:** docs/product/PRD.md:260 (every screen has an empty state that says what to do next); PRD.md:291-298 (the internal console's screens and what each allows); silent on a console landing: recommendation
- **Best fix:** Add /console as the internal roles' home and make '/' send every internal role there: one page of what needs a person now, each a count linking to its queue and filtered view: new audit requests, open tickets (and the oldest's age against the response target), support grants open and ending within the hour, specialist work waiting (documents, proposals, questions, regulations), organizations past due or suspended, maintenance and any component not operational, release drafts and checks. Show each block only to the roles that can act on it (platform administrator, support, contract specialist, reviewer). Make 'Internal console' in the rail a link to it with the single active underline. Move 'New organization' below the directory, or behind a 'New organization' button, since a converted audit request already creates the group.
- **Notes:** Counts only: no PHI, no practice record. The counts reuse each page's existing query (console_support_grants(), the specialist queue's counts, prospect_request by status, support tickets by status, billing standing). shell.tsx, help.ts and routes/console.ts are in #42.

### `flows-other-04`: Leaving a practice for an account, organization, support or terms page drops the practice: the rail loses its screens and the switcher reads 'Choose a practice'

- **Flags:** should_fix; size M; touches #42
- **Screens:** `support.default.1280`, `support.help.1280`, `support.ticket-opened.1280`, `account.devices.default.1280`, `account.notifications.default.1280`, `account.notifications.saved.1280`, `settings.billing.default.1280`, `settings.billing-org.default.1280`, `settings.users.default.1280`, `settings.users.firm.1280`, `terms.default.1280`
- **Routes:** `/support`, `/account/devices`, `/account/notifications`, `/settings/billing`, `/settings/billing/[organizationId]`, `/settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users`, `/terms`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/lib/http/invoke.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/http/next.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/support/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/terms/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users/page.tsx`
- **Evidence:** Every non-practice route renders the shell with currentPracticeId null: run() passes running.practiceId, which self, platform and organization routes leave null (apps/web/src/lib/http/invoke.ts:220-227,265), so ShellNav omits the whole Practice nav (apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx:169) and the switcher shows 'Choose a practice' (shell.tsx:57). A billing manager who clicks 'Help with this screen' > 'Ask support about this screen' on Remittances lands on /support?from=remittances (shell.tsx:339-342) with four account links and no way back to Remittances but the header switcher (shots support.default.1280, support.help.1280). The practice administrator reaches 'Users and roles' from the practice's Settings (apps/web/src/components/settings/nav.tsx:23) and arrives on a page with no Settings nav, no Practice nav and no rail item marked current, because the page's section 'users' has no rail link (apps/web/src/app/settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users/page.tsx:35; shell.tsx:281-293). The Settings nav's 'Notification preferences' (nav.tsx:25) leaves the practice the same way. /support and /terms use section 'home', so 'Help and support' is never marked current (apps/web/src/app/support/page.tsx:31; apps/web/src/app/terms/page.tsx:27; shell.tsx:291 hard-codes active={false}).
- **Rule:** docs/product/PRD.md:260 (navigation is a left rail with the practice switcher at the top; one practice at a time); PRD.md:256 (no screen depends on client-side state a refresh loses: the fix must be server-side)
- **Best fix:** Keep the practice the person came from on every non-practice page. When a /p/<id> page renders, set an HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict cookie holding that opaque practice ID; shellFor reads it for self, organization and platform routes and uses it as the current practice only if the actor still reaches it (practiceDirectory already lists those), so the rail keeps the practice's screens, the switcher names the practice, and a refresh keeps both. Give Users and roles its rail entry under the organization (see flows-other-05) and mark 'Help and support' current on /support (a 'support_request' section). The ticket form shows which screen it is about ('About: Remittances') with a link back to it.
- **Notes:** The cookie holds an opaque ID only (no PHI, CLAUDE.md 'Never'), and access is re-checked on every request, so a revoked grant simply falls back to 'Choose a practice'. shell.tsx and the users page are in #42.

### `flows-other-05`: The organization's administrator has no organization area: users hide in one practice's Settings, billing in the account links, and both lose the way back

- **Flags:** should_fix; size M; touches #42; in flight: #135 the bank account shown first (billing pages; PR #47): the billing list and page changes here should land with or after it
- **Screens:** `settings.billing.default.1280`, `settings.billing.firm.1280`, `settings.billing-org.default.1280`, `settings.users.default.1280`, `settings.users.firm.1280`, `settings.users.notice-invited.1280`, `console.specialist-queue.default.1280`, `account.devices.internal-shell.1280`
- **Routes:** `/settings/billing`, `/settings/billing/[organizationId]`, `/settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/http/route.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/http/invoke.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/settings/billing/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/billing.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/settings/nav.tsx`
- **Evidence:** The administrator's two organization tasks sit in different places: 'Users and roles' only as a Settings sub-link of whichever practice is open (apps/web/src/components/settings/nav.tsx:23), and 'Billing' as an account link shown to every signed-in person (apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx:288-290), including viewers, contract specialists and platform administrators, who only reach its empty state (route-map.json /settings/billing notes; shots console.specialist-queue.default.1280 and account.devices.internal-shell.1280 show 'Billing' in their rails). An administrator of one organization clicks Billing, gets a list holding one card with no standing on it, and clicks again to see the standing (apps/web/src/app/settings/billing/page.tsx:25-49; shots settings.billing.default.1280, settings.billing.firm.1280). On Users and roles the invite form, the task the page exists for, sits below every member row (users/page.tsx:97-110; with ten members it starts about 1,400 px down, settings.users.default.1280), and the 'Done: Invitation sent' notice appears at the top while the new invitation is listed at the foot (settings.users.notice-invited.1280).
- **Rule:** docs/product/PRD.md:282 (Settings: users and roles, grants and agreements, billing; administrator-only actions marked); silent on where organization pages live: recommendation
- **Best fix:** Give an organization's administrator an 'Organization' section in the rail, named with the organization, shown only to roles holding user.invite or organization.billing: 'Users and roles' and 'Billing' (and, for a firm, its clients). Show 'Billing' only to those roles; everyone else loses a link that leads nowhere. When the person administers one organization, /settings/billing opens that organization's billing directly; with several, each card shows its standing. On Users and roles, put an 'Invite someone' button at the top that opens the form, and after inviting show the new invitation beside the notice.
- **Notes:** The shell needs the actor's organization roles (it carries platform roles today), which the organization directory already resolves. shell.tsx and the users page are in #42.

### `flows-other-06`: 'Help with this screen' on Support, Terms and the support engineer's home shows the home help, which tells customers about the staff console and staff about a switcher they lack

- **Flags:** should_fix; size S; touches #42
- **Screens:** `support.help.1280`, `terms.help.1280`, `home.help.1280`, `console.organizations.help.1280`, `console.organization.help.1280`, `console.support.help.1280`
- **Routes:** `/support`, `/terms`, `/`, `/console/organizations`, `/console/organizations/[organizationId]`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/components/help.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/support/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/terms/page.tsx`
- **Evidence:** The help box prints HELP[section] (apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx:335-338). /support and /terms render with section 'home' (apps/web/src/app/support/page.tsx:31; apps/web/src/app/terms/page.tsx:27), so a billing manager on Support and a practice administrator on Terms read 'Your starting point: pick a practice in the switcher, or open the console if you work for PaidWhole.' (apps/web/src/components/help.ts:9), which describes neither page and names the internal console to a customer; on Support the box also offers 'Ask support about this screen' (shell.tsx:339-342) while the person is already on Support. The support engineer on '/' gets the same text with no switcher and no practice (home.help.1280). The organization page reuses the organizations list's help 'Directory records only.' (help.ts:27; console.organization.help.1280), nothing about its six cards; for staff, 'Ask support about this screen' opens a customer ticket to their own desk.
- **Rule:** docs/product/PRD.md:256 (every error shown with a next step: help that names steps the person cannot take misleads); silent on per-screen help text: recommendation
- **Best fix:** Give each page its own help key and text: 'support' for the customer's Support page (what a ticket is, the response target, what never to send), 'terms' for Terms (what accepting records and where the document is), 'home_internal' for a staff member with no practice (where their queue is), 'organization' for the console organization page (its cards in order). Hide 'Ask support about this screen' on /support itself and for internal roles, who work the desk. Add a unit test that every ShellSection has its own entry and no customer-facing entry names the console.
- **Notes:** help.ts and shell.tsx are in #42: land after it. The words lens may hold the wording; this finding is the mapping of help to page.

### `flows-other-07`: A support ticket loses its context on the way in and its answer on the way out: the desk cannot reply in the product and the customer cannot read the reply

- **Flags:** should_fix; size L; needs a migration; touches #42
- **Screens:** `support.default.1280`, `support.ticket-opened.1280`, `console.tickets.default.1280`, `console.tickets.all.1280`
- **Routes:** `/support`, `/console/tickets`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/support/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/support/[ticketId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/support.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/console/tickets/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/tickets/actions.ts`, `apps/worker/src/platform/support-notices.ts`
- **Evidence:** 'Ask support about this screen' sends only the screen's section (from=remittances; apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx:339-342), which the form keeps in a hidden field and never shows (apps/web/src/app/support/page.tsx:50), so the customer is told to copy 'the record's ID from its address' by hand (support/page.tsx:43-45). The desk sees 'screen: remittances' and the message (apps/web/src/app/console/tickets/page.tsx:69-73) and can only 'Mark answered', 'Mark closed' or 'Reopen' (tickets/page.tsx:76-87): there is no reply box and no mailto, so the answer is written in someone's mail client and the product never records what was said or when. The customer's 'Your tickets' lists number, subject, opened and status (support/page.tsx:62-83): an 'answered' ticket cannot be opened to read the message or the answer. Nothing shows a ticket's age against the published target on the desk.
- **Rule:** docs/product/PRD.md:256 (every error shown with a next step); combined audit #103 (an accessibility contact that opens a support ticket depends on this loop); silent on ticket replies: recommendation
- **Best fix:** Close the loop inside the product. (1) The help link carries the page's path with its opaque IDs (never a name or claim number; CLAUDE.md 'Never'), checked against the route registry, and the form shows 'About: Remittances, remittance 3f2a…' with a link back. (2) Each ticket on the desk gets a reply box: the reply is screened for patient information as the ticket is, mailed from the support address through the existing outbox, stored on the ticket, and marks it answered; a reply from the customer's mail reopens it. (3) 'Your tickets' opens a ticket to its thread. (4) The desk's list shows each open ticket's age against the target and sorts the oldest first.
- **Notes:** A ticket_reply table (or reply columns) needs a migration and Lane A; whether ticket text is in the PHI registry decides its practice_id and RLS. shell.tsx and apps/worker/src/platform/support-notices.ts are in #42.

### `flows-other-08`: Support engineers and contract specialists cannot ask for a support grant in the product, and the administrator re-types the ticket into a free-text field

- **Flags:** should_fix; size M; needs a migration; touches #42
- **Screens:** `console.specialist-queue.default.1280`, `console.specialist-queue.all.1280`, `console.support.default.1280`, `console.support.ended.1280`, `console.tickets.default.1280`
- **Routes:** `/console/specialist-queue`, `/console/support`, `/console/tickets`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/app/console/specialist-queue/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/tickets/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/support/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/console.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/support.ts`
- **Evidence:** A practice with a document awaiting abstraction and no grant shows 'No support grant: ask a platform administrator for one from the ticket' (apps/web/src/app/console/specialist-queue/page.tsx:106; console.specialist-queue.default.1280, first row: 1 awaiting, no link), though such work arrives from an upload, not a ticket; the specialist has no button to ask. The support engineer's ticket row has no 'Request access' either (apps/web/src/app/console/tickets/page.tsx:76-87). The platform administrator's form takes the ticket as free text matching [A-Za-z0-9-]{1,64} and the practice from a list of every active practice (apps/web/src/app/console/support/page.tsx:50-57; apps/web/src/lib/routes/console.ts:683,733; console.support.default.1280: 'Ticket' is an empty text field, 'SYNTHETIC OTHER PRACTICE' preselected), so nothing ties a grant to a real ticket and the operator must find the practice by name. The grants table lists open and ended grants together, newest first, up to 100 with no paging (console.ts:682), and an ended grant differs from an open one only by a small outline badge (console.support.ended.1280). The specialist queue's default 'work waiting' view also lists six practices whose every count is 0 because they hold a grant (console.specialist-queue.default.1280).
- **Rule:** docs/product/PRD.md:298 (Support: open support grants; new grant from a ticket); PRD.md:296 (Specialist queue: abstract, verify, approve, ask); silent on how a grant is requested: recommendation
- **Best fix:** Make the request part of the work. On the specialist queue and on each ticket, a 'Request access' action names the practice (the ticket's organization's practices, or the queue row's practice) and the reason; it appears on /console/support as a pending request at the top, with the ticket linked and the practice preselected, and the platform administrator grants or declines it there (the two-person control stays: the requester never grants). The grant form's Ticket becomes a choice of open tickets (or 'Specialist work, no ticket' with a required reason) instead of free text. List open grants first, ended ones under 'Ended in the last 7 days'. The queue's default view shows only practices with work waiting; 'Show every practice' shows the rest.
- **Notes:** A pending grant request needs somewhere to live (a table or a ticket state): migration, Lane A. apps/web/src/app/console/support/page.tsx and routes/console.ts are in #42.

### `flows-other-09`: The console's widest-reaching actions fire on one click with no confirmation, while the customer's End membership asks for a tick

- **Flags:** should_fix; size M; touches #42
- **Screens:** `console.organization.default.1280`, `console.organization.suspended.1280`, `console.status.default.1280`, `console.status.maintenance-on.1280`, `console.models.default.1280`, `console.screening.default.1280`, `console.screening.approved.1280`, `console.billing.default.1280`, `console.support.default.1280`, `settings.users.end-membership-ticked.1280`
- **Routes:** `/console/organizations/[organizationId]`, `/console/status`, `/console/models`, `/console/screening`, `/console/billing`, `/console/support`
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/app/console/organizations/[organizationId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/status/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/models/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/screening/split-form.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/billing/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/console/support/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/console.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/billing.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/support.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/routes/console-models.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/screening/routes.ts`
- **Evidence:** Each of these posts at once from a single button with hidden fields only: 'Suspend the organization', the page's first card (apps/web/src/app/console/organizations/[organizationId]/page.tsx:65-73); 'End membership' on every member row (same file :160-165), which revokes that person's sessions at once; 'Turn maintenance on', which closes every customer screen (apps/web/src/app/console/status/page.tsx:49-52); 'Turn every model feature off for everyone' (apps/web/src/app/console/models/page.tsx:60-63); 'Approve this split', which changes the place-of-service split of every practice's next run and the screening (apps/web/src/components/screening/split-form.tsx:92); 'Issue audit invoice', which charges $2,500.00 to the organization preselected as the first in the list, here SYNTHETIC BILLING FIRM (apps/web/src/app/console/billing/page.tsx:80-83; console.billing.default.1280); and 'End now' on a support grant (apps/web/src/app/console/support/page.tsx:99). The customer's Users and roles, by contrast, requires 'They lose access now' ticked before End membership (apps/web/src/app/settings/organizations/[organizationId]/users/page.tsx:85).
- **Rule:** docs/product/PRD.md:302 (every destructive action confirms with the consequence stated, and is reversible where the domain allows)
